    Below are some ways to write CSS.
    ;jo CSS ,shk wdldr lsysmhla my;ska oelafõ'

    1. Inline CSS
       Inline CSS is used to apply styles directly to an HTML element using the style attribute.
       Inline CSS Ndú;d lrkafka style Wm,laIK Ndú;fhka HTML uQ,dx.hlg fl,skau fudaia;r
       fh§ugh'

       <p style="color: blue; text-align: center;">
       This is an inline styled paragraph.</p>

    2. Internal CSS
       Internal CSS is used to apply styles within the <style> tag in the <head> section of an HTML
       document.
       HTML f,aLkhl <head> fldgfia <style> Wiq,kh ;=< fudaia;r fh§ug wNHka;r CSS Ndú;d
       lrhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -70-                                          #ictfromabc

         <html>
         <head>
           <style>
             p {
                color: blue;
                text-align: center;

           </style>
         </head>
         <body>
           <p>This is an internally styled paragraph.</p>
         </body>
         </html>

    3. External CSS
         External CSS is used to apply styles by linking an external stylesheet file using the <link> tag in the
         <head> section of an HTML document.
         HTML f,aLkhl <head> fldgfia we;s <link> Wiq,kh Ndú;fhka ndysr stylesheet f.dkqjla
         iïnkaO lsÍfuka ú,di fh§ug ndysr CSS Ndú;d lrhs'

         <!-- HTML Document -->
         <html>
         <head>
           <link rel="stylesheet" type="text/css" href="styles.css">
         </head>
         <body>
           <p>This is an externally styled paragraph.</p>
         </body>
         </html>

         /* styles.css */
         p {
           color: blue;
           text-align: center;

    The order of precedence in CSS, from highest to lowest, is.
    CSS ys m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj," by< isg my< olajd my; oelafõ'

    1.   Inline styles (highest) fma<s.; fudaia;r ^by<u&
    2.   ID selectors ID f;aÍï
    3.   Class, attribute selectors mka;sh" Wm,laIK f;aÍï
    4.   Type and pseudo-element selectors j¾.h iy jHdc uQ,dx. f;aÍï
    5.   Universal selector and combinators ish,a, f;aÍï iy ixfhdack

    Let's break down why this is the case.
    fuh tfia jkafka ukaoehs wms fidhd n,uq.

    1. The CSS code provided defines two style rules.
       imhd we;s CSS fla;h ú,di kS;s folla ks¾jpkh lrhs'
         One for the body element
         body element i|yd tlla
         One for elements with the class highlight

        •   Text color to yellow

            Font family to Arial, Cambria iu. fallback f,i

 48. Consider the following HTML code line related to a form:

    2) specifies the server file that handles the data in the form.

    wms tla tla úl,am ms<snoj i,ld n,uq'

    1. Specifies the data type of the form.
       fmdarufha o;a; j¾.h meyeÈ,sj olajhs'
       False. The action attribute does not specify the data type of the form. It specifies the URL to send the
       form data to.
       wi;H fõ' action Wm,laIKh fmdarufha o;a; j¾.h i|yka fkdlrhs' th fmdaru o;a; heùug
       URL tl i|yka lrhs'

       True. The action attribute in an HTML form specifies the URL of the server-side script (in this case,
       process.php) that will handle the data submitted by the form. When the form is submitted, the data
       entered in the form fields will be sent to the specified file on the server for processing.
       i;H fõ' HTML fmdaruhl we; action Wm,laIKh fmdaruh u.ska bÈßm;a lrk ,o o;a; yiqrejk

       fj; hjkq ,efí'

       Alignment is controlled by CSS or other HTML attributes.
       wi;H fõ' action Wm,laIKhg fjí msgqfõ we;s fmdaruh fm<.eiaùu iuÕ lsisÿ iïnkaOhla
       ke;' fm<.eiau CSS fyda fjk;a HTML Wm,laIKh u.ska md,kh fõ'

 49. Saman’s father is a carpenter. He wants to showcase his father’s work on a website. Which of the
     following hosting options should Saman use in order to do it with a price that he can afford?
     iukaf.a mshd jvq ld¾ñlfhls' fjí wvúhl" ;u mshdf.a ks¾udK m%o¾Ykh lsÍug iuka woyia lrhs'

    2) Hosting it on a Virtual Private Server (VPS)

    Answer : 1 )

    Web hosting is a critical service that enables individuals and organizations to publish their websites on the
    internet. Different types of web hosting cater to various needs, budgets, and technical requirements. Below
    are the primary types of web hosting services:
    fjí fydiaáka i;Hu jYfhkau jeo.;a fiajdjla jk w;r mqoa.,hka iy ixúOdkj,g Tjqkaf.a fjí
    wvú wka;¾cd,hg m%ldYhg m;a lsÍug yelsfõ' úúO fjí fydiaáka j¾. úúO wjYH;d" whjeh
    iy ;dlaIKsl wjYH;d imqrd,Su i|yd ;sfí' my; oelafjkafka m%Odk fjí fydiaáka fiajd j¾.hhs

    1. Shared Web Hosting - wxYsl fjí fydiaáka
       Shared hosting is the most economical option, where multiple websites share a single server and its
       resources. This type is ideal for beginners and small websites due to its low cost. However, shared
       hosting has limitations, such as slower loading times and restricted customization options. The
       performance of one site can be affected by others on the same server, especially during high traffic
       periods.
       wxYsl fydiaáka wvq msßjeh iys;j we;s jvd;au wd¾Ólsl úl,amh jk w;r" fndfyda fjí wvú
       tlu fiajdodhlh iy tys iïm;a fnod .kS' fuu j¾.h w¨;ska wdrïN lrk whg iy l=vd
       fjí wvú i|yd iqÿiqhs' flfia fj;;a" wxYsl fydiaáka iSud we;" WodyrKhla f,i iq¿l, o;a;
       Ndrlh iy wNsreÑlrK úl,am iSud we;' tla fjí wvúhl ld¾hidOkh ;j;a fjí wvú j,
       m%;smdokh u; n,mEï t,a, fõ" úfYaIfhkau jeä m%jdyh we;s wjia:d j,§'

    2. Virtual Private Server Hosting - w;:H mqoa.,sl fiajdodhl fydiaáka ^VPS&
       VPS hosting provides a middle ground between shared and dedicated hosting. Although multiple
       websites share the same physical server, virtualization technology allocates dedicated resources (like
       RAM and CPU) to each site. This setup offers better performance and stability compared to shared
       hosting, making it suitable for medium-sized businesses or websites with moderate traffic. VPS
       hosting requires some technical knowledge to manage effectively.
       VPS fydiaáka wxYsl iy mßmQ¾K fydiaáka w;r ueo miqneiSï ,nd foa' tfiau fyda tlu fN!;sl

       ld¾h idOk iy ia:dhS;dj ,nd foa" uOHu mßudK jHdmdr i|yd fyda uOH mßudK fjí wvú
       i|yd iqÿiqhs' VPS fydiaáka md,kh lsÍug l=ulafoda ;dlaIKsl oekqu wjYH fõ'

    3. Dedicated Server Hosting mßmQ¾K fiajdodhl fydiaáka
       With dedicated server hosting, a single server is rented exclusively for one website. This option offers
       maximum performance, security, and control, making it suitable for large websites with high traffic
       volumes. However, it comes at a higher cost and requires technical expertise to manage the server
       effectively.
       mßmQ¾K fiajdodhl fydiaáka iu." tla fjí wvúhla i|yd muKla mßmQ¾K fiajdodhlhla

    4. E-commerce site
       E-commerce sites are often not considered "hosting" in the traditional sense because these sites are
       pre-built web sites where we can list our product but not customize the site as much as we want.
       E-Commerce wvú idïm%odhsl “fydiaáka” f,i i,lkq fkd,efí' ukao kï fuu wvú fmr-we;s
       fjí wvú jk w;r wmg wmf.a ksIamdokh ,hsia;=.; l< yels kuq;a wvúh wNsreÑlrKh lr
       fkdyels fõ'

    5. Cloud Hosting j<dl+¿ fydiaáka
       Cloud hosting utilizes a network of virtual servers that draw resources from a centralized pool. This
       flexibility allows for scalable resources, meaning websites can handle traffic spikes without
       performance issues. Cloud hosting is often billed based on usage, making it a cost-effective option for
       businesses that experience fluctuating traffic levels. It's important to note that the term "cloud hosting"
       can vary between providers, so it's essential to understand what is included in the service.
       j,dl=¿ fydiaáka uOHiaÓ; l=,l%uhg iïm;a ,nd .kakd w;:H fiajdodhl cd,hla Ndú;d lrhs'

    Option 03
    An IoT (Internet of Things) sensor is a device that detects and measures a specific physical or

    over the internet. In basic terms, an IoT sensor acts as the "eyes and ears" of the IoT system, gathering
    data from the real world and making it available for analysis and action. It is considered to be the primary
    role of the IoT devices.

 1. The <form> Tag
    <form method= "post" action= "./action_page.php">
    The <form> tag is used to create an HTML form for user input. The action attribute indicates the URL to
    which the form data will be sent upon submission. In this question, it points to action_page.php in the
    same directory. The method attribute specifies how the form data should be sent. In this case, post means
    the data will be sent in the body of the HTTP request, which is suitable for sensitive data.
    <form> Wiq,kh mßYS,l wdodkh i|yd HTML fmdaruhla ks¾udKh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs. action
    Wm,laIKfhka oelafjkafka bÈßm;a lsÍfuka miq fmdaru o;a; hjkq ,nk URL tlhs' fuu m%Yakfha§"
    th tu kdudj,sfhysu action_page.php fj; fhduq lrhs' method Wm,laIKh uÕska fmdaru o;a;
    heúh hq;= wdldrh i|yka lrhs' fuu wjia:dfõ§" post hkq ixfõ§ o;a; i|yd iqÿiq HTTP l%uhg
    o;a; hjkq we;'

 2. Input Text Field
    <input type="text" name="name">
    The <input> element in HTML represents an input field where the user can enter data. The text
    attribute specifies that this input field should accept plain text input. The name attribute assigns the name
    "name" to the input field, which is useful for identifying the data when it is submitted to the server.
    HTML ys <input> uQ,dx.h mßYS,lhdg o;a; we;=<;a l< yels wdodk lafIa;%hla ksfhdackh lrhs'
    text Wm,laIKh fuu wdodk lafIa;%h text wdodk ms<s.; hq;= nj i|yka lrhs' name Wm,laIKh

    m%fhdackj;a fõ'

 3. Radio Buttons
    <input type ="radio" name="gender" id="male" value="male">
    The <input> element with type="radio" creates a radio button that allows the user to select one option
    from a group. The name attribute groups the radio button with others that share the same name, ensuring
    that only one option can be selected at a time within that group. The id attribute assigns a unique identifier
    to the radio button, which can be used to label it or reference it in scripts. The value attribute specifies the
    value that will be submitted to the server if this radio button is selected. Overall, this code represents a
    radio button for selecting "male" as a gender option.
    type="radio" iys; <input> Wiq,kh f¾äfhda fnd;a;ula idok w;r tuÕska lKavdhulska tla
    úl,amhla f;dard .ekSug mßYS,lhdg bv i,ihs' name Wm,laIKh f¾äfhda fnd;a;u tlu ku fnod

    fyda th ial%smaÜ j, i|yka lrhs' value Wm,laIKh fuu f¾äfhda fnd;a;u f;dard we;akï

 4. The Checkbox
    < input type= "checkbox" name="newsletter" id="newsletter">
    The <input> tag with type=checkbox creates a checkbox that allows users to make a binary choice select
    or deselect. The name attribute assigns a name to the checkbox, which is used to identify the data when
    the form is submitted to the server.When the checkbox is checked, the value associated with the name
    attribute will be sent to server upon form submission. If the checkbox is unchecked, no value will be sent.
    <input type= “checkbox”> uÕska mßYS,lhskag oaúuh f;aÍula lsÍug fyda f;aÍfuka bj;a lsÍug bv

 7. The Submit button
    <input type= "submit" value= "submit">
    The <input type= “submit”> creates a button that, when clicked, submits the form data to the server.
    type="submit" specifies that the input is a submit button, which triggers the form submission process.
    value attribute defines the text displayed on the button. In this case, the button will show "submit" to user.
    <input type= “submit”> fnd;a;ula idohs" th la,sla l< úg" fmdaruh o;a; fiajdodhlhg bÈßm;a

 8. The Image tag
    <img src = "wpeLogo.jpg" alt= "WPElogo" width="50" height="60">
    The <img> tag in HTML is used to insert an image into a web page.
    Src attribute specifies the source or path of the image file. In this case, the image file is named
    "wpeLogo.jpg" and is located in the same directory as the HTML file.Alt This attribute provides an
    alternative text description of the image. It is used for accessibility purposes, such as when the image
    cannot be displayed or when a user is using a screen reader.Width and height attributes set the width and
    height of the image in pixels. They can be used to resize the image without affecting the original image
    file.In summary, this code inserts an image of the "WPElogo" with a width of 50 pixels and a height of 60
    pixels into the web page.
    HTML ys <img> Wiq,kh fjí msgqjlg rEmhla we;=¿ lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'Src .=Kdx.h rEm f.dkqfõ

    <?php
    $servername = "localhost"; $username = "root"; $password = "";
    $dbname = "environment";
    // Create a connection
    $conn = new mysqli($servername, $username, $password, $dbname);

    // Section P
    $name = $_POST['name']; $gender = $_POST['gender']); $district = $_
    POST['district']; $email = $_POST[‘email']; $newsletter = $_POST(['news-
    letter'];
    // section P end
    // Section Q
    $sql = "INSERT INTO applicants (name, gender, district, email,
    newsletter) VALUES ('$name', '$gender', '$district', '$email',
    '$newsletter')";
    // section Q end
    if ($conn->query($sql) === TRUE) {
          echo "Data inserted successfully!";
    } else {
          echo "Error: " . $sql . "<br>" . $conn->error;

    // Close the connection
    mysqli_close($conn);

   $_POST Method & $_GET Method
   The $_POST method is used to send data to the server for processing, typically when submitting forms that
   create or update resources. The data is sent in the body of HTTP request, making it more secure than GET.
   $_POST l%uh idudkHfhka iïm;a idok fyda hdj;ald,Sk lrk wdlD;s m;% bÈßm;a lsÍfï§" ieliSu
   i|yd fiajdodhlh fj; o;a; heùug Ndú;d lrhs' o;a; HTTP b,a,Sfï body fldgig hjkq ,efí"
   th GET l%uhg jvd wdrlaIs; lrhs'

   The $_GET method is used to retrieve data sent via the URL. When a form is submitted using the GET
   method, the form data is appended to the URL as query parameters. This method is suitable for requests
   where data retrieval is needed without modifying server state.
   GET yryd hjk ,o o;a; ,nd .ekSug $_GET l%uh Ndú;d lrhs' GET l%uh Ndú;fhka fmdaruhla
   bÈßm;a l< úg" fmdaru o;a; úuiqï mrdñ;s f,i URL fj; tla flf¾' fuu l%uh fiajdodhl
   ;;a;ajh fjkia fkdlr o;a; ,nd .ekSu wjYH jk b,a,Sï i|yd iqÿiq fõ'

2. (a) A simple and high-level view of the data lifecycle consists of three steps. Write the 2nd and the 3rd
       steps of the data life cycle.
       ir, f,i iy by< uÜgulska o;a; Ôjk pl%h (data life cycle) neÆ úg" th mshjr ;=klska iukaú;
       fõ' tys fojk yd f;jk mshjr ,shd olajkak'

     The data life cycle refers to the stages that data goes through from its creation to its eventual disposal.
     Understanding the data life cycle helps organizations manage and utilize their data effectively. There are
     3 main steps here.
     o;a; Ôjk pl%h hkq o;a; ks¾udKh lsÍfï isg wjidkfha neyer lsÍu olajd .uka lrk wjëka h'

     data governance, data integration, data quality, and data security.

    E-waste, or electronic waste, refers to discarded electronic devices and components such as computers,
    smartphones, televisions, and other electronic appliances. These items often contain hazardous materials
    like lead, mercury, and cadmium, which can harm the environment and human health if not properly
    disposed of. E-waste recycling involves reclaiming valuable materials and safely managing toxic
    substances to reduce environmental impact and promote sustainability.

    n,mEu wju lsÍu iy ;sridr nj m%j¾Okh lsÍu i|yd jákd øjH kej; ,nd .ekSu iy úI øjH
    wdrlaIs;j l<ukdlrKh lsÍuhs'

3. (a) Write down the most suitable replacements for labels A to G in the following flowchartewhich is
       drawn to calculate and display the sum of first ten even numbers.
       m<uq brÜfÜ ixLHd oyfha tl;=j .Kkh lr m%o¾Ykh lsÍu i|yd we| we;s my; .e,Sï igyfka
       A isg G f;la f,an, i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq wdfoaYl ,shd olajkak'

                        Start

                      sum = 0

                     count = 0
                                                           A – No
                                                           B – Yes
             A                         B                   C – sum=sum+count / sum += count
                      Is count
                       even?

                                           C               E – count <21 / count<=20

                 count = count +1

                 D                                         E – count >20 / count >=21
                          E                                F – Yes

                              F

                         G

                        End

     What needs to happen here is to check if count is an even number and if it is true then add it to the sum
     variable and add 1 to the count variable. If the test is false, 1 should be added directly to the count variable.
     In the loop represented by this flowchart, the initial value of count, the control variable, is 0. The beginning
     of the loop can be identified as where an arrowhead hits when moving from top to bottom. Accordingly,
     its condition should be below the point where the arrowhead hits or where the arrowhead is received. Here,
     after the arrowhead hit, there is no condition related to repetition. Therefore, arrows should move to the
     place where the head is. Then the relevant condition should be given there. Here the sum of the first 10
     even numbers should be calculated. The first 10 even numbers end in 20. Therefore, if the value of count
     is less than 21, then the loop should go back up and if not, that is, if the value of count is more than 20,
     sum should be output and the flowchart should be terminated. Accordingly, the answers to the blanks in
     the above flowchart are as follows.

     Let's study the given code line by line.
     ,nd § we;s fla;fha fma<sfhka fma<sh wOHkh lruq'

     for i in range(4,n+1):

                 MyNumber.append(i)

     Here a for loop is mentioned. where 4 to n+1 (including 4 and excluding n+1) is substituted for its control
     variable i at each iteration. Then in the loop, it checks if the value of i%2 is equal to 0. Then that value is
     appended to the MyNumber list. i%2 means the remainder when i is divided by 2. If nothing is left then it
     is an even number and if 1 is left then it is an odd number. Accordingly, it can be recognized that what
     happens in this code is to calculate even numbers from 4 to a given number, append them to a list, and
     output them.

   Functional Requirements - l%shdnoaO wjYH;d

   Functional requirements define what a system is supposed to do.
   l%shdnoaO wjYH;d moaO;shla l< hq;= foa ks¾jpkh lrhs'

   They describe the specific behaviors or functions of a system. These are the features and functions that
   the system must deliver to meet the user's needs.
   Tjqka moaO;shl ksYaÑ; yeisÍï fyda ld¾hhka úia;r lrhs' mßYS,lhdf.a wjYH;d imqrd,Su i|yd
   moaO;sh iemhsh hq;= úfYaIdx. iy ld¾hhka fïjdh'

   Non-Functional Requirements - l%shdnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d

   Non-functional requirements describe how the system performs a function, rather than what function
   it performs. They usually address aspects such as performance, security, usability, reliability, etc.
   l%shdnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d úia;r lrkafka moaO;sh th bgq lrk ld¾hhg jvd ld¾hhla bgq lrk
   wdldrhhs' Tjqka idudkHfhka ld¾h idOkh" wdrlaIdj" Wmfhda.S;dj" úYajikSh;ajh hkdÈh wduka;%Kh
   lrhs'

   •   The system shall handle up to 10,000 simultaneous users.

   According to the question, it is asking about functional requirement with respect to computer reservations.

(c) Give one technical aspect that the development team should check when conducting the technical
    feasibility study of this project.
    fuu jHdmD;sfha ;dlaIKsl YlH;d (technical feasibility) wOHhkh isÿ lrk úg" ixj¾Ok lKavdhu
    úiska mÍlaId l< hq;= tla ;dlaIKsl wx.hla ,shd olajkak.

   First, let’s discuss about the different type of feasibility studies available.
   m<uqj" mj;sk úúO wdldrfha YlH;d wOHhk l%u ms<snoj idlÉPd lruq'

   Technical Feasibility - ;dlaIKsl YlH;dj
   Assesses the availability of technology, resources, and expertise to implement the project. It examines
   whether the existing technology, infrastructure, and expertise are sufficient to develop and implement
   the project. This includes evaluating software, hardware, technical skills, and production capabilities.
   jHdmD;sh l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd ;dËKh" iïm;a iy m%ùK;ajh ,nd .ekSfï yelshdj ;lafiare lrhs'
   jHdmD;sh ixj¾Okh lsÍug iy l%shd;aul lsÍug oekg mj;sk ;dlaIKh" há;, myiqlï iy
   m%ùK;ajh m%udKj;ao hkak th mÍlaId lrhs' uDÿldx." oDvdx." ;dlaIKsl l=i,;d iy ksIamdok
   yelshdjka we.hSu fuhg we;=<;a fõ'

   Economic Feasibility - wd¾Ól YlH;dj
   Analyzes the financial viability, including costs, revenues, profitability, and return on investment .
   This study involves cost-benefit analysis, estimating the total cost of the project (including
   development, implementation, and maintenance costs) and comparing it with the expected financial
   benefits or returns.

    lsÍu i|yd" ksrjoH wjYH;d úYaf,aIKhla b;d jeo.;a jkafka wehs?
   The Waterfall model is a classic project management methodology where project phases are completed
   sequentially, with each phase depending on the deliverables of the previous one. This linear flow resembles
   a waterfall.
   Èhwe,s wdlD;sh hkq iïNdjH jHdmD;s l<ukdlrK l%ufõohla jk w;r tys§ jHdmD;s wÈhrhka

   .,dhdu Èh we,a,lg iudkhs'

   In the waterfall model, each stage must be completed before the next phase can begin.Accordingly, if the
   requirement analysis is not carried out properly at that time, it is possible to develop a system that will be
   rejected by the customers. Correcting that mistake is very expensive and will take time.
   Èhwe,s wdlD;sfha" B<Õ wÈhr wdrïN lsÍug fmr iEu wÈhrlau iïmQ¾K l< hq;=h'fï wkqj wjYH;d

   Software Testing - uDÿldx. mÍlaIdj
   Software testing is a critical process to ensure that a software application functions as expected, meets user
   requirements, and is free from defects. It involves executing a program with the intent of finding errors.
   uDÿldx. mÍlaIKh hkq uDÿldx. fhÿula wfmalaIs; mßÈ l%shd lsÍu" mßYS,l wjYH;d imqrd,Su iy
   fodaI j,ska f;dr nj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd ;SrKd;aul l%shdj,shls' fodaI fiùfï wruqKska jevigykla
   l%shd;aul lsÍu thg we;=<;a fõ'

   Types of Software Testing
   uDÿldx. mÍlaIK j¾.

   1. Based on the software development lifecycle
      uDÿldx. ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%h u; mokïj
       •   Unit Testing - tAll mÍlaId lsÍu
           Testing individual components or units of code.
           ;ks ixrpl fyda fla; tAll mÍlaId lsÍu'

       •   Integration Testing - tAldnoaO;d mÍlaIdj
           Testing how different components interact with each other.
           úúO ixrpl tlsfkld iuÕ wka;¾ l%shd lrk wdldrh mÍlaId lsÍu'

       •   System Testing - moaO;s mÍlaIdj
           Testing the entire system as a complete product.
           iïmQ¾K ksIamdokhla f,i iuia; moaO;shu mÍlaId lsÍu'

       •   Acceptance Testing - ms<s.ekSfï mÍlaIKh
           Verifying if the software meets the user's requirements.
           uDÿldx.h mßYS,l wjYH;d imqrd,kafka oehs mÍlaId lsÍu'

   2. Based on the testing technique
      mÍlaIK ;dlaIKh u; mokïj
       •   Black-box Testing – ld, uxcqid mÍlaIdj
           Testing the software without knowing its internal structure.
           tys wNHka;r jHqyh fkdoek uDÿldx.h mÍlaId lsÍu'

       •   White-box Testing – Yafõ; uxcqid mÍlaIdj
           Testing the software with knowledge of its internal structure.
           uDÿldx.h tys wNHka;r jHqyh ms<sn| oekqfuka mÍlaId lsÍu'

   After performing the unit test of the three modules, the modules were integrated into a single intergrated
   unit. Testing this intergrated units as a unified unit is carried out in the integration test.

(f) The IT teacher suggests the team doing a direct deployment of this software. Give one reason as to why
    the teacher did not suggest a parallel deployment.
    IT .=re;=ñh fuu uDÿldx.h iDcq ia:dmkh (direct deployment) lsÍug lKavdhug fhdackd lrhs' weh
    iudka;r ia:dmkh (parallel deployment) fhdackd fkdlsÍug tla fya;=jla olajkak.

   First let’s discuss about the four types of system deployment methods.
   m%:ufhkau moaO;s ia:dmkh l%u y;r .ek idlÉPd lruq'

   •   Parallel Deployment - iudka;r ia:dmkh
       Both the old and new systems run simultaneously for a period. Users can work with both
       systems until the new one is confirmed to be working correctly. It safe, as the old system remains
       available if issues arise with the new system.
       merKs iy kj moaO;s folu ld, mßÉfþohla i|yd tljr lS%hd;aul fõ' kj tl ksjerÈj
       l%shd lrk nj ;yjqre jk ;=re mßYS,lhskag moaO;s folu iuÕ jev l< yel' kj moaO;sh
       iuÕ .eg¿ we;s jqjfyd;a merKs l%uh mj;sk neúka th wdrlaIs;hs'

   •   Phased Deployment - wÈhruh ia:dmkh
       The new system is implemented in stages or phases. Each phase may introduce a part of the new
       system or roll it out to a portion of users. Allows for gradual transition and easier troubleshooting.
       kj l%uh l%shd;aul jkafka wÈhr fyda wÈhr jYfhka h' iEu wÈhrl§u kj moaO;sfha fldgila
       y÷kajd §ug fyda mßYS,lhska fldgilg th fmr<Sug yelsh' l%ufhka ixl%uKh ùug iy fodaY
       ksrdlrKhg myiq ùug bv i,ihs'

   •   Pilot Deployment - ia:dmkh
       The new system is first deployed to a small group of users (a pilot group) to evaluate its
       performance. Based on the results, the system is then deployed to the rest of the users. Allows
       testing and feedback before a full rollout, reducing risks.
       kj moaO;sh uq,skau tys ld¾h idOkh we.hSu i|yd l=vd mßYS,lhska lKavdhula ^kshuq
       lKavdhula& fj; fhdojkq ,efí' m%;sM, u; mokïj" moaO;sh miqj b;sß mßYS,lhska fj;
       fhdojkq ,efí' wjodku wvq lrñka"iïmQ¾K fmr<shlg fmr mÍlaIK iy m%;sfmdaIKhg bv fohs'

   •   Direct Deployment - kshuq ia:dmkh
       The old system is completely replaced by the new system in one go. All users transition to the new
       system at the same time. Quick transition with no need to maintain both systems simultaneously.
       me/Ks l%uh iïmQ¾Kfhkau kj l%uhg tl mdrg wdfoaY fõ' ish¨u mßYS,lhska tlu wjia:dfõ§u
       kj moaO;shg ixlaruKh fõ' moaO;s folu tljr kv;a;= lsÍug wjYH fkdjk blauka ixl%dka;sh'

   In this question, the most suitable deployment method for the above software is direct deployment.
   Below are the reasons why parallel deployment being not suitable here.
   fuu m%Yakfha§" by; uDÿldx.h i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq fhojqï l%uh jkafka iDcq fhoùuhs' iudka;r
   ia:dmkh fuys fkd.e,fmk fya;= my; oelafõ'

       moaO;s folla kv;a;= lsÍug jeä ld,hla .;fõ'
   •   Maintaining two systems is more expensive.
       moaO;s folla kv;a;= lsÍu jvd ñ, wêlh'
   •   Effort must be made to keep the two systems consistent.
       moaO;s fol wkql+,j (consistent) ;nd .ekSug mßY%uhla oeßh hq;=h'
   •   It can cause confusion and frustration.
       th jHdl+,;ajh yd l,lsÍu we;s l< yelsh'
   •   Since it is only for the use of the passel, any error is relatively easy to be resolved. Therefore, parallel
       installation is not necessary.
       th mdfi,a Ndú;h i|ydu muKla jk neúka" ´kEu fodaIhla idfmalaIj myiqfjka úi|d .;
       yel' tuksid iudka;r ia:dmkh wjYH ke;'
   •   This is not a high-risk system that requires parallel installation.
       fuh iudka;r ia:dmkhla wjYH jk wê wjodkï moaO;shla fkdfõ'

5. (a) A circuit with three inputs (A, B, C) and one output (Z) is to be designed. The output should be equal to
       1 when the binary value combination of the three inputs is either 1, 3 or 6. The output should be 0 for
       other cases.
       A, B iy C kï jQ wdodk ;=klska yd Z kï jQ tla m%;sodkhlska iukaú; mßm:hla ks¾udKh l<
       hq;=j we;' wdodk ;=fka oaúuh w.hkaf.a ixfhdackh 1 fyda 3 fyda 6 fyda jk úg" m%;sodkh 1 úh
       hq;= h' wfkl=;a wjia:d i|yd m%;sodkh 0 úh hq;= h'

          (i) Draw the complete truth table for the above circuit.
              by; mßm:h i|yd iïmQ¾K i;H;d j.=j w¢kak'

              The output should be equal to 1 when the binary value combination of the three inputs is either 1, 3 or
              6. The output should be 0 for other cases.
              wdodk ;=fka oaúuh w.h ixfhdackh 1, 3 fyda 6 jk úg m%;sodkh 1 g iudk úh hq;=h' wfkl=;a
              wjia:d i|yd m%;sodkh 0 úh hq;=h'

              So, the truth table is as follows;

    (ii) Complete the Karnaugh map relevant to the above circuit according to the following format.
         by; mßm:hg wod< ldfkda is;shu my; wdlD;sh mßÈ iïmQ¾K lrkak.
       First, let’s map ICNs.
       m<uqj" wms ICN is;shï.; lruq'

       Now, let’s complete the K-map with 1/0 value for the relevant ICNs.
       oeka" wms wod< ICN i|yd 1/0 w.hka u.ska ldfkda is;shu iïmQ¾K lruq'

    (iii) Using the Karnaugh map, derive the most simplified product-of-sums (POS) expression for
          the output Z. Show the loops clearly on the Karnaugh map.
          Z m%;sodkh i|yd tl;+kaf.a .=Ks; (product-of-sums) jvd;a ir,;u m%ldYh ldfkda is;shu
          Ndú;fhka jHq;amkak lrkak' Æm (loops) meyeÈ,s f,i ldfkda is;shfï fmkajkak.

       To derive the most simplified product-of-sums (POS) expression using a K-map:
       ldfkda is;shu Ndú;fhka jvd;a ir, lrk ,o tl;=fjys ^POS& m%ldYkh jHq;amkak lsÍug(
       •   Identify and group the 0s ^0 y÷kdf.k lKavdhï lrkak&
           In a K-map, focus on grouping the cells that contain 0s. Each group of zeros will correspond to a
           sum term in the POS expression.
           ldfkda is;shul" 0 wvx.= ffi, ldKav.; lsÍu flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrkak' iEu ìxÿ
           iuQyhlau POS m%ldYkfha tl;=jlg wkqrEm fõ'
       •   Form the sum terms ^tl;= lsÍfï mo idokak&
           For each group of zeros (0s), form a sum term within that group.
           tla tla 0 iuQyhla i|yd" tu lKavdhu ;=< tl;= lsÍfï mohla idokak'
       •   Combine the sum terms ^tl;= lsÍfï mo tAldnoaO lrkak&
           Combine all the sum terms by multiplying them to get the final POS expression.

                                                ̅C + BC is equivalent to C + A

 (e) (i) Write two functions of a proxy server in a computer network.
         ksfhdað; fiajdodhlhl (proxy server) m%Odk ld¾hka folla ,shd olajkak.

       A Proxy Server is an intermediary server that sits between a client (such as a web browser) and another
       server (like a web server). It acts as a gateway, facilitating requests from the client and forwarding them
       to the appropriate server, and then returning the response from the server back to the client. Proxy
       servers are used for a variety of purposes, including privacy, security, caching, and controlling access
       to resources.
       Proxy Server hkq fiajd,dNsfhl= ^fjí n%jqirhla jeks& iy ;j;a fiajdodhlhla ^fjí fiajdodhlhla

       •   Acting as an intermediary between the user's computer and the Internet / Providing access to the

           level of privacy / Users' real IP addresses hiding

    (ii) Write two properties of MAC addresses assigned to devices connected to a network.

       A MAC Address (Media Access Control Address) is a unique identifier assigned to a network interface
       controller (NIC) for communications at the data link layer of a network segment. It is used in various
       networking technologies, including Ethernet, Wi-Fi, and Bluetooth, to identify devices on a local
       network.

    (i) Software Agents demonstrate certain characteristics which make their behaviour unique. Briefly
        explain the following two characteristics of a software agent:
        uDÿldx. ldrl ;u yeisÍï wkkH lrkakd jQ .;s,laIK m%o¾Ykh lr;s' uDÿldx. ldrlhl

       (a) autonomous iajdëk
       (b) cooperative iyfhda.S
       The explanation of the two characteristics of a software agent mentioned above is as follows.
       by; i|yka l< uDÿldx. ldrlfhl=f.a ,laIK foflays meyeÈ,s lsÍu my; mßÈ fõ'

       (a) Autonomous - iajdëk
           Software agents are described as autonomous because they can operate independently without
           human intervention. They make decisions and take actions based on predefined rules and
           algorithms. This independence allows agents to adapt to changing conditions and respond to
           new information in real time, enhancing system efficiency and flexibility.
           uDÿldx. ldrlhka iajhxl%Sh f,i úia;r lr we;af;a Tjqkag ñksia ueÈy;aùulska f;drj

           iy kuHYS,S nj jeä lsÍug bv i,ihs'

       (b) Cooperative - iyfhda.S
           Software agents are considered cooperative because they can work together with other agents
           to achieve common goals. Information and tasks are shared among agents to optimize
           processes and outcomes. This cooperation is facilitated through communication protocols and
           mutual goals, enabling agents to coordinate their actions effectively within a system
           fmdÿ wruqKq idlaId;a lr .ekSu i|yd fjk;a ksfhdað;hka iuÕ tlg jev l< yels neúka

(b) The function in Figure 8.2 uses the bubblesort algorithm to sort a given list of numbers into ascending
    order. Write down the suitable replacements for the labels P-U to complete the code.
    rEmh 8.2 ys oelafjk my; mhs;ka Y%s;h" fok ,o wxl ,ehsia;=jla wdfrdayK l%uhg ieliSug nqnq<M
    f;aÍï (bubble sort) we,af.dß;uh Ndú; lrhs' fla;h iïmQ¾K lsÍu i|yd P – U f,an,j,g iqÿiq
    wdfoaYl ,shd olajkak'

       def bubblesort(nList):                                P       len(nList)-1
           for pnumber in range(P,Q,R):                      Q       0
               s:                                            R       -1
                if nList[i] > nList[i+1]:                    S       for i in range(pNumber):
                   temp = nList[i]
                      T                                      T       nList[i] = nList[i+1]
                      U                                      U       nList[i+1] = temp

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -15-                                          #ictfromabc

   Alternative for P, Q, R and S
    P     0
    Q     len(nList) OR len(nList)-1
    R     1
    S     for i in range(0, len(nList)-1)
          OR
          for i in range(len(nList)-1)
          OR
          for i in range(0, len(nList)-1-pNumber)

   Understanding the Bubble Sort Algorithm - Bubble Sort we,af.dß;u wjfndaO lr .ekSu
   Bubble sort is a simple sorting algorithm that repeatedly steps through the list, compares adjacent elements,
   and swaps them if they are in the wrong order. This process is repeated until no swaps are needed, indicating
   that the list is sorted.
   Bubble sort hkq ,ehsia;=j yryd kej; kej;;a mshjr .kakd" hdno uQ,dx. ixikaokh lrk iy tAjd
   jerÈ wkqms<sfj,l ;sfí kï tAjd udre lr ir, f,i j¾. lsÍfï we,af.dß;uhls' ,ehsia;=j
   wkqms<sfj,g ilia lr we;s nj wÕjñka" yqjudrejla wjYH fkdjk f;la fuu l%shdj,sh kej; isÿ
   flf¾'
   •   P, Q, R
       The outer loop iterates over the entire list for each pass. The starting index is 0 (first element), the
       ending index is the length of the list minus 1 (last element), and the step value is 1 (increment by 1 for
       each iteration).
       msg; Æmh tla tla jghla i|yd iïmQ¾K ,ehsia;=j u; mqkrdj¾;kh fõ' wdrïNl o¾Ylh 0
       jk (m<uq uQ,dx.h&" wjidk o¾Ylh hkq ,ehsia;=fõ È. iDK 1 ^wjidk uQ,dx.h& jk w;r step
       w.h 1 fõ ^tla tla mqkrdj¾;kh i|yd 1 lska jeä lsÍu&'
   •   S
       This inner loop compares adjacent elements within a pass. It iterates from the beginning of the list to
       the second-to-last element (index i).
       fuu wNHka;r Æmh B<Õ mqkrdj¾;khg hdfï§ hdno uQ,dx.h iuÕ ixikaokh lrhs' th
       ,ehsia;=fõ wdrïNfha isg wjidk fojk uQ,dx.h (index i). olajd mqkrdj¾;kh fõ'
   •   T and U
       These lines perform the swap of adjacent elements if they are in the wrong order.
       fuu f¾Ldj hdno uQ,dx. jerÈ wkqms<sfj,l ;sfí kï tAjd yqjudre lsrsu isÿ lrhs'
   1. T: The value at index i+1 is assigned to the variable temp.
      i+1 o¾Ylfha w.h úp,H temp g mjrd we;'

   2. U: The value of temp (which was originally at index i+1) is assigned to the index i, effectively
      swapping the elements.
      temp ys w.h ^uq,ska i+1 o¾Ylfhys mej;s& i index fj; mjrkq ,efí" M,odhs f,i uQ,dx.
      fjkia lrhs'
   In Summary
   The provided replacements accurately implement the bubble sort algorithm. The outer loop controls the
   number of passes, the inner loop compares adjacent elements, and the T and U lines perform the necessary
   swaps to sort the list in ascending order.
   imhd we;s wdfoaYkh bubble sort we,af.dß;u ksjerÈj l%shd;aul lrhs' msg; Æmh mdia .Kk md,kh
   lrhs" wNHka;r Æmh hdno uQ,dx. ixikaokh lrhs" iy T iy U f¾Ld ,ehsia;=j wdfrdayK
   wkqms<sfj,g j¾. lsÍug wjYH yqjudre lsÍï isÿ lrhs'
   def bubbleSort(nList):
     for pNumber in range(0, len(nList)-1, 1):
       for i in range(pNumber):
         if nList[i] > nList[i+1]:
           temp = nList[i]
           nList[i] = nList[i+1]
           nList[i+1] = temp

        if netpay < 0:
            continue

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -19-                                          #ictfromabc

         print("\n")
         print(empDetails[0],"Net pay =",netpay)
         topay = netpay
         i = 0
         while topay > 0:
             required[i] = topay // notes[i]
             totals[i] = totals[i] + required[i]
             topay = topay % notes[i]
             i += 1

         # print employee netpay breakdown
         for i in range(0, len(required)):
             print("Rs.",notes[i],":", required[i])
         file.close()

   print("\nTOTAL REQUIREMENT: ")
   for i in range(0, len(totals)):
       print("Rs.",notes[i],":",totals[i])

   Answers : )
   A : open
   B : not OR "" == OR " == (with no space between quotes)
   C : break
   D : empDetails[1]
   E : topay//notes[i]
   F : required[i]
   G : topay%notes[i] OR topay - required[i]*notes[i]
   H: i=i+1
   I : required[i]
   J : file.close()

    (ii) The net pay of employees in this estate, does not contain cents. However, what practical problem with
         respect to the net pay inputs exists in this code? What modifications will you do to fix that problem?
         fuu j;=hdfha fiajlhkaf.a Y=oaO jegqmaj, Y; .Kka wvx.= fkdfõ' tfy;a Y=oaO jegqma wdodk

       This code can only handle net payments with the notes provided in the notes array, payments that does
       not divide by 50 or 20 cannot be handled because in the given array all the notes are a factor of 50 or
       20 so payments that doesn’t belong to them cannot be handled.
       fuu fla;hg fkdaÜgq wrdfõ imhd we;s fkdaÜgq iuÕ Y=oaO f.ùï muKla yeisrúh yel" 50 fyda
       20 ka fnfokafka ke;s f.ùï yeisrúh fkdyel" ukao ,nd § we;s wrdfõ ish¨u fkdaÜgq 50 fyda

  (b) Write two advantages of converting a database table into a normal form.
      o;a; iuqod j.=jla m%u; wjia:djlg (normal form) yerùfï jdis folla ,shd olajkak'
      •   Elimination of Redundancy and Data Anomalies - w;sßla;h iy o;a; úIu;d bj;a lsÍu
      •   Improved Data Integrity and Consistency - jeäÈhqKq l< o;a; wLKav;dj iy wkql+,;dj
      •   Enhanced Query Performance - jeä ÈhqKq l< úuiqï ld¾h idOk
      •   Better Scalability- jvd fyd| mßudK
      •   Easier Maintenance and Updates - myiq kv;a;= iy hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu
      •   Improved Security - jeä ÈhqKq l< wdrlaIdj
      •   Data Integrity Enforcement Through Constraints - iSudjka yryd o;a; wLKav;dj n,d;aul lsÍu
      •   Facilitates Data Migration and Integration - o;a; ixl%uKh iy tAldnoaO lsÍu myiq lrhs
      •   Reduced Data Duplication - wvq l< o;a; wkqmsgm;a

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -21-                                          #ictfromabc

   Elimination of Redundancy and Data Anomalies - w;sßla;h iy o;a; úIu;d bj;a lsÍu
   •   Redundancy: By converting a database table into a normal form, you minimize redundant data. This
       means that the same piece of data is not unnecessarily duplicated across multiple rows, which saves
       storage space and makes data management more efficient.
       w;sßla;h( o;a; iuqod j.=jla m%u:lrK wdlD;shlg mßj¾;kh lsÍfuka" Tn w;sßla; o;a; wju
       lrhs' fuhska woyia lrkafka tlu o;a; lene,a,la fma<s lsysmhla yryd wkjYH f,i wkqmsgm;a
       fkdjk w;r tuÕska .nvd bv b;sß jk w;r o;a; l<ukdlrKh jvd;a ld¾hlaIu fõ'
   •   Anomalies: Normalization helps eliminate anomalies in the database. For instance, without
       normalization, operations such as insertions, updates, or deletions can lead to inconsistent data states
       (insertion, update, and deletion anomalies). Normalization ensures that data integrity is maintained.
       úIu;d( m%u:lrKh o;a; iuqodfha úIu;d bj;a lsÍug WmldÍ fõ' ksoiqkla f,i" m%u:lrKhlska
       f;drj" we;=<;a lsÍï" hdj;ald,Sk lsÍï fyda uldoeóï jeks l%shd fya;=fjka wkdl+=, o;a; ;;a;ajhka
       ^we;=<;a lsÍu" hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu iy uld oeófï úIu;d& we;s úh yel' m%u:lrKh o;a;
       wLKav;dj mj;ajd f.k hk nj iy;sl lrhs'

   Improved Data Integrity and Consistency - jeäÈhqKq l< o;a; wLKav;dj iy wkql+,;dj
   •   Integrity: Normal forms enforce data integrity rules that ensure the accuracy and reliability of the data
       within the database. For example, in higher normal forms, relationships between tables are clearly
       defined, reducing the risk of mismatched data.
       wLKav;dj( m%u:lrK wdlD;s o;a; iuqodh ;=< o;a;j, ksrjoH;djh iy úYajikSh;ajh iy;sl
       lrk o;a; wLKav;d kS;s n,d;aul lrhs' WodyrKhla f,i" by< m%u:lrK wdldrj,§" j.= w;r
       iïnkaO;d meyeÈ,sj ks¾jpkh lr we;s w;r" fkd.e,fmk o;a; wjodku wvq lrhs'
   •   Consistency: With a well-normalized database, changes made to data in one place are automatically
       propagated throughout the database, ensuring consistency. This means that all views and reports
       derived from the database reflect the most current data, leading to more accurate decision-making.
       wkql+,;dj( fyd¢ka m%u:lrKh lrk ,o o;a; iuqodhla iuÕska" o;a; .nvdj mqrd tl ;ekl isÿ
       lrk ,o fjkialï iajhxl%Shj m%pdrKh jk w;r" wkql+,;dj iy;sl lrhs' fuhska woyia lrkafka
       o;a; iuqodfhka ,nd.;a ish¨u o¾Yk iy jd¾;d jvd;a j¾;udk o;a; ms<sìUq lrk w;r th
       jvd;a ksjerÈ ;SrK .ekSug u. mdohs'

   Enhanced Query Performance - jeä ÈhqKq l< úuiqï ld¾h idOkh
   By reducing redundancy and organizing data into related tables, normalized databases can improve query
   performance. Indexes can be more effectively used, and queries can be more efficient because the database
   engine has fewer rows to scan and join operations are simplified.
   w;sßla;h wvq lsÍfuka iy wod< j.=j,g o;a; ixúOdkh lsÍfuka" m%u:lrKh l< o;a; iuqodhka
   úuiqï ld¾h idOkh jeä ÈhqKq l< yel' o¾Yl jvd;a M,odhs f,i Ndú;d l< yels w;r" o;a;
   iuqod tkaðug ialEka lsÍug iy iïnkaO ùug fma<s wvq neúka úuiqï jvd;a ld¾hlaIu úh yels w;r
   fufyhqï ir, lr we;'

   Better Scalability - jvd fyd| mßudKh
   A normalized database is generally easier to scale. As the database grows, maintaining it becomes less
   cumbersome because the data is logically organized and free from unnecessary duplication. This makes it
   simpler to add new data, tables, or relationships without disrupting the existing structure.
   m%u:lrKh l< o;a; iuqodhla idudkHfhka mßudKh lsÍug myiqh' o;a; iuqodh j¾Okh jk úg"
   o;a; ;d¾lslj ixúOdkh ù we;s w;r wkjYH wkqmsgm;a j,ska f;dr neúka th kv;a;= lsÍu wmyiq
   fõ'mj;sk jHqyhg ndOd fkdlr kj o;a;" j.= fyda iïnkaO;d tl;= lsÍu fuu.ska myiq lrhs

   Easier Maintenance and Updates - myiq kv;a;= iy hdj;ald,Sk
   When a database is normalized, maintaining and updating it becomes more manageable. Schema changes,
   such as adding new fields or modifying existing ones, can be done with minimal impact on the overall
   database structure. This flexibility makes it easier to adapt to changing business requirements.
   o;a; iuqodhla m%u:lrKh jQ úg" th kv;a;= lsÍu iy hdj;ald,Sk lsÍu jvd;a l<ukdlrKh l<
   yels fõ' kj lafIa;% tl;= lsÍu fyda mj;sk tAjd fjkia lsÍu jeks fhdackd l%u fjkia lsÍï" iuia;
   o;a; iuqod jHqyhg wju n,mEula iys;j isÿ l< yel' fuu kuHYS,SNdjh fjkiajk jHdmdßl
   wjYH;dj,g wkq.; ùu myiq lrhs'

            groups or arrays.

       •   Full Functional Dependency - iïmQ¾K l%shdldÍ hemSu
           Every non-key attribute must be fully functionally dependent on the entire primary key, not just a

10. (a) (i) What is the repeating cycle that a processor in a computer is involved in since the computer is started
            till it is shutdown?
            mß.Klh mK.kajd l%shdúrys; lrk ;=re" mß.Klfha ilikh (processor) kej; kej; ksr;jk
            pl%h (repeating cycle) l=ula o?
          fetch-decode-execute cycle / instruction cycle
          wdyrk ls%hd lrjqï pl%h $fijqï bIaG pl%h
          The fetch-decode-execute cycle (or instruction cycle) is the basic operational process of a CPU.
          wdyrk l%shd lrjqï pl%h$fijqï bIaG pl%h fyda Wmfoia pl%h hkq CPU tll uQ,sl fufyhqï l%shdj,shhs'
          1. Fetch

             CPU úiska jevigyka ljqkagrfha (PC) ,smskh Ndú;d lrñka u;lfhka Wmfoia ,nd .kS'
          2. Decode
             The CPU decodes the fetched instruction to understand what actions are required.
                    s d fudkjdo hkak f;areï .ekSug CPU úiska ,nd.;a Wmfoia úfla;kh lrhs'
             wjYH l%h
          3. Execute
             The CPU performs the required actions, such as calculations, data transfer, or control operations.
             CPU úiska .Kkh lsÍï" o;a; yqjudrej fyda md,k fufyhqï jeks wjYH l%shd isÿ lrhs'
          This cycle repeats continuously as long as the computer is powered on.
          mß.Klh l%shd;aul jk ;dla fuu pl%h wLKavj kej; isÿ fõ'

    (ii) Which program's instructions get executed in the processor of a computer during a context switch?
         ixo¾N iaúphla (context switch) w;r;=r§ mß.Klhl ilikfha l%shd;aul jkafka l=uk
         l%uf,aLfha (program) Wmfoia o?

       Operating system
       fufyhqï moaO;sh

       Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu

       During a context switch, the processor executes instructions from the operating system’s context-
       switching code.

       l%shd;aul lrhs

          Block (PCB).

          íf,dla (PCB) fj; iqrlshs'

          The operating system updates its data structures to reflect the current process's new state.

          The CPU loads the context of the next process from its PCB.
          CPU tys PCB fj;ska B<Õ l%shdj,sfha ikao¾Nh mQrKh lrhs'

       4. Update Memory Management - hdj;ald,Sk u;l l<ukdlrKh
          The operating system updates memory management settings as needed.
          fufyhqï moaO;sh wjYH mßÈ u;l l<ukdlrK ieliqï hdj;ald,Sk lrhs'

       5. Resume Execution - kej; l%shd;aul lsÍu
          The CPU resumes execution with the next process's context.
          CPU B<Õ l%shdj,sfha ikao¾Nh iuÕ l%shd;aul lsÍu kej; wdrïN lrhs'

   (iii) A register is a group of binary cells suitable for holding binary information and is constituted by a
         collection of flip-flops. How many flip-flops are needed to make an n-bit register?
         frðia;rhla (register) hkq oaúuh f;dr;=re r|jd ;nd .ekSug iqÿiq oaúuh fldaI (binary cells)
         iuQyhla jk w;r th ms<s-fmd< (flip-flop) tl;=jlska iukaú; fõ' ìgq n iys; frðia;rhla
         iE§u i|yd wjYH ms<s-fmd< .Kk fldmuK o?
       n
       Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
       A flip-flop is a basic memory element that can store one bit of data (either 0 or 1). An n-bit register is
       designed to hold n bits of data. Therefore, to store each of these n bits, you need one flip-flop per bit.
       Hence, n flip-flops are required to create an n-bit register. Each flip-flop in the register corresponds to
       one bit, allowing the register to store n bits of information simultaneously.
       flip-flop hkq o;a; ìgq tlla ^0 fyda 1) .nvd l< yels uQ,sl u;l wx.hls' n-bit f,aLkhla ks¾udKh
       lr we;af;a n ìgq o;a; r|jd ;nd .ekSu i|yd h' tuksid" fuu tla tla n ìgq .nvd lsÍu i|yd"
       Tng ìgq tllg tla ms<sfmd< tlla wjYH fõ' tneúka" n-bit f,aLkhla iE§ug ms<sfmd< n ixLHdjla
       wjYH fõ' f,aLkfha we;s iEu ms<sfmd<lg wkqrEm jk w;r" f,aLkhg n ìgq f;dr;=re tljr
       .nvd lsÍug bv i,ihs'

     by; mhs;ka l%shdhk fol w;ßka tll" wfklg jvd, l%shd;aul → wjysr l< hk l%shdhk ixl%dka;sh

 (d) Assume that when the fileReader process of (b) above is in progress a context switch occurs and a
     different process is run. When the fileReader process is given the chance to run again, the file is read
     from where it stopped. Which data structure facilitates that feature?
     by; (b) ys fileReader l%shdhkh Odjkh jk úg ixo¾N iaúphla isÿ ù fjk;a l%shdhkhla Odjkh
     fõ hehs i,lkak' fileReader l%shdhkhg Odjkh lsÍug kej; wjia:dj ,nd ÿka úg" keje;a jQ ;ek
     isg f.dkqj lshjhs' fuu .=Kdx.h myiq lrkafka l=uk o;a; jHqyh o?
     Process Control Block(PCB) in fileReader
     fileReader ys l%shdj,s md,k jdrKh (PCB).

     Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
     The Process Control Block (PCB) facilitates reading a file from its stopped position by storing crucial
     information about the process, including the state of open files and their associated file descriptors. During
     a context switch, the PCB saves the process state, which includes the file descriptor and its current file
     position. When the process resumes, the operating system restores the PCB, which ensures that the file
     descriptor retains its position in the file, allowing the process to continue reading from where it left off.
     l%shdj,s md,k íf,dla (PCB) újD; f.dkqj, ;;a;ajh iy tAjdg iïnkaO f.dkq úia;r lsÍï we;=¿j
     l%shdj,sh ms<sn| ;SrKd;aul f;dr;=re .nvd lsÍfuka f.dkqjla tys k;r jQ ia:dkfha isg lshùug
     myiqlï imhhs' ikao¾Nh udre lsÍfï§" PCB f.dkq úia;rlh iy tys j;auka f.dkq ;;a;ajh we;=<;a
     l%shdj,s ;;a;ajh iqrlshs' l%shdj,sh kej; wdrïN jQ úg" fufyhqï moaO;sh PCB m%;sidOkh lrhs"
     tuÕska f.dkq úia;rlh f.dkqj ;=< tys msysàu r|jd ;nd .ekSu iy;sl lrhs" th l%shdj,sh k;r
     l< ;ek isg lshùu È.gu lrf.k hdug bv i,ihs'

 (e) A computer uses 32-bit virtual addresses. This computer has a 1 GB (230 bytes) physical memory and a
     4 KB page size.
     mß.Klhla ìgq 32 l w;:H fhduq Ndú; lrhs' fuu mß.Klfha fN!;sl u;lfha úYd,;ajh 1GB
     (230 bytes) f,i;a msgqjl (page) úYd,;ajh 4 KB f,i;a oelafõ.

     (i) Write down the number of frames in physical memory as a power of 2.
         fN!;sl u;lfha we;s rduq (frame) .Kk 2 ys n,hla f,i ,shd olajkak.
        218
        Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
        Number of Frames in Physical Memory: With a 1 GB (2³⁰ bytes) physical memory and a page size of
        4 KB (2¹² bytes), the number of frames is calculated by dividing the total memory by the page size.
        Therefore,
        fN!;sl u;lfha we;s rduq .Kk( 1 GB (2³⁰ nhsg) fN!;sl u;lhla iy msgq m%udKh 4 KB (2¹²
        nhsg& iuÕska" rduq .Kk .Kkh lrkq ,nkafka msgq m%udKfhka iïmQ¾K u;lh fn§fuks' tneúka"
        No . of frames = 230 / 212 = 218

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -31-                                           #ictfromabc

    (ii) Assume that in addition to memory frame information, each page table entry for a virtual page in this
         computer contains some additional information consisting of a total of four bits. If the total size of the
         page table required for each process on this computer assuming that all virtual pages are in use is
         given as 2p x q bits, write down the values of p and q.
         fuu mß.Klfha msgq j.=fõ (page table) w;:H msgqjlg wod< igyfka (page table entry) u;l
         rduq f;dr;=rej,g wu;rj, ìgq 4 l È.lg ;j;a f;dr;=re o we;ehs Wml,amkh lrkak' ish¨u
         w;:H msgq (virtual page) Ndú; lr;ehs Wml,amkh l< úg" fuu mß.Klfha tla l%shdhkhla
         i|yd wjYH msgq j.=fõ iuia; úYd,;ajh ìgq 2𝑝 × 𝑞 f,iska oelafõ kï" 𝑝 iy 𝑞 j, w.hka
         ,shkak.
       p = 20 q = 22
       Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
       Page Table Size - msgq j.= m%udKh
       Each page table entry includes additional information of 4 bits, so each entry requires 4 bits for this
       information plus the bits needed to store the frame number. Since there are 218 frames, the frame number
       requires 18 bits. Thus, each page table entry requires 18+4=22 bits. The virtual address space is 32-bit,
       and with a page size of 4 KB (2¹² bytes), there are 232/212=220 virtual pages. Therefore, the total size of
       the page table is 220 x 22 bits, which simplifies to 2p x q bits. Here, p=20 and q=22.
       iEu msgq j.= m%fõYhlgu ìgq 4l wu;r f;dr;=re we;=<;a fõ" tneúka iEu m%fõYhlgu fuu
       f;dr;=re i|yd ìgq 4la iy rduq wxlh .nvd lsÍug wjYH ìgq wjYH fõ' rduq 218 la we;s neúka"
       rduq wxlhg ìgq 18 la wjYH fõ' fï wkqj" iEu msgq j.= m%fõYhlgu ìgq 18 + 4 = 22 wjYH fõ'
       w;:H ,smsk wjldYh 32-ìÜ jk w;r msgq m%udKh 4 KB ^2¹² nhsÜ& iuÕ 232/212=220 w;:H msgq
       we;' tneúka" msgq j.=fõ iïmQ¾K m%udKh ìgq 220 x 22 jk w;r th 2p x q fõ. tu ksid, p=20 iy
       q=22 fõ.

   (iii) If the virtual address 4097 of a particular process is mapped to Frame 2 of physical memory, write
         down in decimal form, the physical address corresponding to the virtual address 4097.
         hï l%shdhkhl 4097 w;:H fhduqj fN!;sl u;lfha rduq wxl 2 g wkqrEmKh fõ kï" 4097
         w;:H fhduqjg wod< fN!;sl fhduqj (physical address) oYuh wdldrfhka olajkak'
       (Assume that page numbers, frame numbers and addresses begin from 0)
       (msgq wxl" rduq wxl iy fhduq" ìkaÿfjka werfUk nj Wml,amkh lrkak')
       8193
       Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
       Physical Address Calculation fN!;sl ,smskh .Kkh lsÍu
       For a virtual address 4097, first determine the page number and offset. With a page size of 4 KB, the
       offset is 4097 mod 212=4097 mod 4096 = 1, and the page number is 4097/ 212 =1. If this virtual page
       maps to Frame 2, the physical address is calculated by combining the frame number with the offset:
       Physical Address
       w;:H ,smskh 4097 i|yd" m<uqj msgq wxlh iy ´*afiÜ ;SrKh lrkak' msgq m%udKh 4 KB iuÕska"
       ´*afiÜ 4097 mod 212=4097 mod 4096 = 1, jk w;r msgq wxlh 4097/ 212 =1 fõ' fuu w;:H msgqj
       rduqj 2 fj; is;shï.; lrkafka kï" fN!;sl ,smskh .Kkh lrkq ,nkafka rduq wxlh ´*afiÜ
       iuÕ tAldnoaO lsÍfuks( fN!;sl ,smskh
       = ( 2 × 212 ) + 1 = 8192 + 1 = 8193. Thus, the physical address corresponding to the virtual address
       4097 is 8193.
       = ( 2 × 212 ) + 1 = 8192 + 1 = 8193 . fï wkqj" w:;H ,smskhg wkqrEm jk fN!;sl ,smskh 4097 hkq
       8193 ls'

     (i) Write down an important number in the directory entry for the test.py file that will help the operating
         system to find the blocks of the file.
         test.py f.dkqjg wod< ldKav fidhd.ekSug fufyhqï moaO;shg bjy,a jk" tu f.dkqjg wod<
         kdudj,s f;dr;=frys (directory entry) we;s jeo.;a wxlhl w.h ,shd olajkak.
        218
        Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
        In a File Allocation Table (FAT) system, the directory entry for a file contains various pieces of
        information, including the starting block of the file. This starting block number is crucial as it helps the
        operating system locate the first block of the file and subsequently traverse the FAT to find all the
        blocks used by the file.
        f.dkq fjka lsÍfï j.= (FAT) moaO;shl" f.dkqjla i|yd jk kdudj,s m%fõYh f.dkqfõ wdrïNl
        fldgi we;=¿j úúO f;dr;=re wvx.= fõ' fuu wdrïNl jdrK wxlh ;Srkd;aul jkafka th
        fufyhqï moaO;shg f.dkqfõ m<uq fldgi fidhd .ekSug iy miqj FAT yryd f.dkqj Ndú;d lrk
        ish¨u fldgia fidhd .ekSug Wmldr jk neúks'

        For the file test.py, the starting block number is important. In this case, the starting block is 218.
        test.py f.dkqj i|yd" wdrïNl jdrK wxlh jeo.;a fõ' fuu wjia:dfõ§" wdrïNl fldgi 218
        fõ'

    (ii) Give an example size for test.py that will result in internal fragmentation.
         wNHka;r LKavkSlrKhg fya;=jk test.py f.dkq úYd,;ajhlg WodyrKhla fokak.
        4 KB < Size < 8 KB
        Explanation - meyeÈ,s lsÍu
        For a file stored on a disk using 4 KB blocks in a FAT system, internal fragmentation occurs when the
        file size is greater than 4 KB but less than 8 KB. This is because a file larger than 4 KB will require
        more than one block, and if its size is not a perfect multiple of the block size, the last block will not be
        fully utilized. For example, a file size of 4500 bytes will use two blocks, with the first block fully
        occupied (4096 bytes) and the second block only partially used (404 bytes), leaving 3692 bytes unused,
        thus causing internal fragmentation. Therefore, the size range for internal fragmentation is 4 KB < Size
        < 8 KB.
        FAT moaO;shl 4 KB íf,dla Ndú;d lrñka ;eáhl .nvd lr we;s f.dkqjla i|yd" f.dkq úYd,;ajh
        4 KB g jvd jeä kuq;a 8 KB g jvd wvq jQ úg wNHka;r LKavkh isÿ fõ' ulaksido h;a" 4 KB g
        jvd úYd, f.dkqjlg íf,dla tllg jvd wjYH jk w;r" tys m%udKh íf,dla m%udKfhka mßmQ¾K
        .=Kdldrhla fkdfõ kï" wjidk fldgi iïmQ¾Kfhkau Ndú;d fkdfõ' WodyrKhla f,i" nhsg
        4500l m%udKhl f.dkqjla íf,dla folla Ndú;d lrkq we;" m<uq fldgi iïmq¾Kfhkau w,a,df.k
        we; ^nhsg 4096& iy fojk fldgi w¾O jYfhka muKla Ndú;d lrhs ^nhsg 404& nhsÜ 3692 la
        Ndú;hg fkdf.k" wNHka;r LKavkh ùug fya;= fõ' tneúka" wNHka;r LKavkh i|yd úYd,
        mrdih 4 KB < m%udKh < 8 KB fõ'

   (iii) Assume that block 219 is also to be added for the test.py file. Show in a diagram the FAT entries for
